FOIPA Request No.: 1319467-000
Subject: ADAMSKI, GEORGE
Dear Mr. Greenewald:
Records responsive to your request were previously processed under the provisions of the
Freedom of Information Act. Enclosed is one CD containing 281 pages of previously processed documents
and a copy of the Explanation of Exemptions. This release is being provided to you at no charge.
Documents or information referred to other Government agencies were not included in this release.
For your additional information, a record that may be responsive to your Freedom of
Information/Privacy Acts (FO|PA) request has been transferred to the National Archives and Records
Administration (NARA). You may desire to direct a request to NARA, 8601 Adelphi Road, College Park, MD
20740-6001. Please reference the file number 100-HQ-395273.
Please be advised that additional records potentially responsive to your subject may exist. If this
release of previously processed material does not satisty your information needs for this request, you may
request an additional search for records. Submit your request by mail or fax to - Work Process Unit, 170
Marcel Drive, Winchester, VA 22602, fax number (540) 868-4997. Please cite the FOIPA Request Number
in your correspondence.
For your information, Congress excluded three discrete categories of law enforcement and national
security records from the requirements of the Freedom of Information Act (FOIA). See 5 U.S. C. § 552(c)
(2006 & Supp. IV (2010). This response is limited to those records subject to the requirements of the FOIA.
This is a standard notification that is given to all our requesters and should not be taken as an indication that
excluded records do, or do not, exist.
For questions regarding our determinations, visit the www.fbi.gov/foia website under "Contact Us."
The FOIPA Request Number listed above has been assigned to your request. Please use this number in all
correspondence concerning your request.
You may file an appeal by writing to the Director, Office of Information Policy (OIP), United States
Department of Justice, Suite 11050, 1425 New York Avenue, NW, Washington, D.C. 20530-0001, or you
may submit an appeal through OIP's FOIA online portal by creating an account on the following web
site: https://foiaonline.regulations.gov/foia/action/public/home. Your appeal must be postmarked or
electronically transmitted within ninety (90) days from the date of this letter in order to be considered timely.
If you submit your appeal by mail, both the letter and the envelope should be clearly marked "Freedom of
Information Act Appeal." Please cite the FOIPA Request Number assigned to your request so it may be
easily identified.

EXPLANATION OF EXEMPTIONS
SUBSECTIONS OF TITLE 5, UNITED STATES CODE, SECTION 552
(A) specifically authorized under criteria established by an Executive order to be kept secret in the interest of national defense or foreign
policy and (B) are in fact properly classified to such Executive order;
related solely to the internal personnel rules and practices of an agency;
specifically exempted from disclosure by statute (other than section 552b of this title), provided that such statute (A) requires that the
matters be withheld from the public in such a manner as to leave no discretion on issue, or (B) establishes particular criteria for withholding
or refers to particular types of matters to be withheld;
trade secrets and commercial or financial information obtained from a person and privileged or confidential;
inter-agency or intra-agency memorandums or letters which would not be available by law to a party other than an agency in litigation with
the agency;
personnel and medical files and similar files the disclosure of which would constitute a clearly unwarranted invasion of personal privacy;
records or information compiled for law enforcement purposes, but only to the extent that the production of such law enforcement records
or information (A) could reasonably be expected to interfere with enforcement proceedings, ( B) would deprive a person of a right to a
fair trial or an impartial adjudication, ( C) could reasonably be expected to constitute an unwarranted invasion of personal privacy, (D)
could reasonably be expected to disclose the identity of confidential source, including a State, local, or foreign agency or authority or any
private institution which furnished information on a confidential basis, and, in the case of record or information compiled by a criminal law
enforcement authority in the course of a criminal investigation, or by an agency conducting a lawful national security intelligence
investigation, information furnished by a confidential source, (E) would disclose techniques and procedures for law enforcement
investigations or prosecutions, or would disclose guidelines for law enforcement investigations or prosecutions if such disclosure could
reasonably be expected to risk circumvention of the law, or (F) could reasonably be expected to endanger the life or physical safety of any
individual;
contained in or related to examination, operating, or condition reports prepared by, on behalf of, or for the use of an agency responsible for
the regulation or supervision of financial institutions; or
geological and geophysical information and data, including maps, concerning wells.
SUBSECTIONS OF TITLE 5, UNITED STATES CODE, SECTION 552a
information compiled in reasonable anticipation of a civil action proceeding;
material reporting investigative efforts pertaining to the enforcement of criminal law including efforts to prevent, control, or reduce crime
or apprehend criminals;
information which is currently and properly classified pursuant to an Executive order in the interest of the national defense or foreign
policy, for example, information involving intelligence sources or methods;
investigatory material compiled for law enforcement purposes, other than criminal, which did not result in loss of a right, benefit or
privilege under Federal programs, or which would identify a source who furnished information pursuant to a promise that his/her identity
would be held in confidence;
material maintained in connection with providing protective services to the President of the United States or any other individual pursuant
to the authority of Title 18, United States Code, Section 3056;
required by statute to be maintained and used solely as statistical records;
investigatory material compiled solely for the purpose of determining suitability, eligibility, or qualifications for Federal civilian
employment or for access to classified information, the disclosure of which would reveal the identity of the person who furnishes
information pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence;
testing or examination material used to determine individual qualifications for appointment or promotion in Federal Government service
he release of which would compromise the testing or examination process;
material used to determine potential for promotion in the armed services, the disclosure of which would reveal the identity of the person
who furnished the material pursuant to a promise that his/her identity would be held in confidence

{"alpha_words": 824, "chars": 4793, "method": "ocr", "page": 11, "variant": "plain-vision"}
To Whom It May Concerg
This
is an inquiry to the individual women who might be interested in adding her
single but effective voice to the concerted effort now being made by many groups
to make an immediate appeal to Congress to STOP FURTHER EXPERIMENTING WITH THE
HYDROGEN BOMB.
felt that our scientists
enough experimenting,
surely, to know
what we have.
We certainly don't need a deadlier weapon.
We hear about islands
being sunk, either intentionally or unintentionally, ashes being scattered over
unanticipated areas, radio-activity possibly contaminating friendly neighbor's
bodies, vegetation and live-stock.
We hear, too,
that the ashes do not get blown
into space but go up to the earth's ceiling-like atmosphere and must, in
We who are interested in this movement do not say that we should throw away the
Peace organizations have tried that and failed because of "Adequate Defense"
Nor should anything be said FOR OR AGAINST whether we should go to war
if we are attacked. If one is interested in writing one's own appeal, or better
still, an appeal from the WHOLE family, listing children's names and ages, it
might be better to use the expression that many groups are using... "Let's put on
the brakes and stop further experimenting with the H bomb".. might be the most
effective.
When a momentum is gotten up in the wrong direction and one wishes to
reverse that momentum...the ONLY thing that can be done is to come to a FULL STOP
BY PUTTING ON THE BRAKES.
NOW... we who are interested in this appeal DO NOT say that we are NOT in favor of
peace. ..BUT...let's
of the ladder and try to climb up.
IF the American people are aroused to the point where their voices must be listened
to...WHO is to say that perhaps the next old-fashioned type war that seems apparently
to be in the offing, if one is to judge by the old familiar pattern of newspaper
and radio and television announcements that are already in evidence, might be averted.
Is it too idealistic to suppose
that if the people who are concerned with such things
see that we mean business about the bomb experimenting...they might, also consider
that we won't sit still for another war, either. AND perhaps, if we stand up to be
heard in this instance (for the first time in many, many years) might it not give
the other peoples of the world, who used to look to the American people for leader-
ship before we (the only nation to do so) dropped the bombs on human beings in
war-fare, courage to stand up,
too. Maybe the peoples of the world can save
themselves from total destruction.
The groups who have been contacted directly are:
Michigan League of Women Voters,
Dearborn Michigan Branch of the AAUW (University Women), Zonta Club (an International
Business Women's group), the YWCA, the PTA, the Detroit Round Table of Christians
and Jews, the Detroit Council of Churches,
the Supt. of Schools in Dearborn,
Michigan and Detroit and the National Education Association through its Michigan
outlet in Lansing... (all local organizations advised to pass a resolution to suggest
to their national parent that an immediate appeal be made by THEM to Congress.)
So far only women's groups (outside of educational sources) have been contacted...
However, there is no attempt to make this exclusively a Mother's March on Science. .
a potential Mothers' League of Nations... If any man,
individually or through
a service-club wishes to chime his voice in with the others, he is certainly invited
to do so.... After all, the men are the fathers and brothers and uncles of the
children to whom we will pass on these unsettled conditions if we do not straighten
them out in our own lifetime.
AND the men are the ones through the years and
millenius of warfare who have fought the battles in good faith while the women
have remained home to weep.
Copies of this letter will go out to an important women's organization in every-
country possible including Russia. If women will take advantage of the freedom that
Science has afforded them by freeing them from their kitchens in most cases, perhaps
we might, for the sake of our children's future welfare, have a voice in saying how
we feel Science should make use of its discoveries.
SO...again it is reiterated that if we as individuals or members of groups or families
get busy and get our appeals off to Congress and say
"Let's put on the brakes and
stop further experimenting with the H bomb"
••• perhaps we'll be able to do that and
get atomic energy converted to the benefit of mankind instead of its destruction...
and only God knows how much more.
Such things as freedom of the press, freedom of petition and the voice of the people
heard throughout the land with the help-of-God can Still bring forth miracles.
Let's get busy on it right away ill I: menelp.
INE MARI CONCRED

name of the Flying Saucer Review Group.

with a meeting to

to issue political propaganda and also bring in a pseudo
religious angle to the program.

far as he can tell this group is definitely against atomic
warfare or the use of atomic bombs by this country.

It will be noted that Mrs. JAN PEARSON, Aviation Writer
tor the Detroit Free Press and a former informant in this matter,
was concerned about remarks that had been made at a previous
meeting of this group to the effect that the U. S. should stop
experimenting with hydrogen bombs because visitors from outer
space were afraid we might be starting something we could not
finish.
As a matter of information to this office, it is believed
that an Agent should attend this meeting to determine the nature of
the statements made.
Attached also is a photostatic copy of a letter which
was not explained by Mr. GOODMAN in his phone call but which was
furnished by him on the same date.
This letter which is signed
with what may be Grecian script and which Is multe lllegible but
which appears to have "JAN" in the signature,
would indicate that
it comes from one who believes he is an individual from outer
space placed here on earth for some unstated purpose.

DESCRIPTION OF SUBJECT:
ALL INFORMATTON CONTAINED
-DATE S
UNCLASSIFIFD
FACTS OF COMPLAINT: Complainant advised that she is employed at the Double Day Bookstore,
Penobscot Bldg., and that she works from 9 a.m. to 6 p.m. every day except Wednesday.
She stated that as a result of her working in the bookstore she had become interested in
Flying Saucers. She stated that one HENRY MADAY who has an office in the Penobscott BIdg.
came into the bookstore to buy books and upon finding out her interest in flying saucers
he invited her to attend a lecture by a person who had written a book on flying saucers.
She stated that she had attended the lecture and also another leature by another person.

The only speaker at the affair was GEORGE ADAMSKI who enlarged on the
aims
and purposes of the Saucer Club as set forth in the above pamphlet. ADAMSKI
was much gratified over the large turnout and stated, "we have present a nucleus
which when organized can go out into the communities and acquaint the public with
the existence of Flying Saucers." He indicated that speakers are needed to
spread the Flying Saucer information.
ADAMSKI lightly touched upon the subject of dues stating dues of a
nominal nature would be expected to obtain literature to be placed
in libraries,
etc.

1954 at 10:30 P.M.

garding Saucers.

MRS. IODISE MOONEY personally contacted the writer at the office on
8-24-54 and advised that she had attended the meeting of the Flying Saucer Club
of Detroit on 8-19-54 at the Veterans Memorial Bldg, Detroit. She had previously
advised the writer that she would attend the meeting.
URS. MOONEY advised that approximately 300 people attended the meeting.
She said that the meeting was mainly concerned with various people giving their
accounts of their sightings of Flying Saucers. After each person gave his
sighting a MR. REEVES, an engineer, would make comments on each one.
According th Mrs. Mooney a MRS. REEVES (see leat'let caption Flying
Saucer club of Detroit in L-A) stated that it was her opinion Irom reading the
books "Flying Saucers have landed" by GEORGE ADAMSKI and "Aboard the Flying
Saucer" by TRUMAN BETHRUM, that the people from makis outer space were trying
to convey to us that the use of the atom bomb was sending a vapor into the
outer space and spoiling their space travel. She said these paspeople from
outer space believed that the vapor would some day descend on earth and
destroy it, and that these people didnot want earth destroyed.
have petitions out for the people to have signed. These petitions are to be
sent to the President of the United states to have him tell the American people
the truth, that there is space travel.
She said that a MR. JOHN HOFFMAN, advertising man, gave a flying saucer
sighting at 15 mile rd. and Stevens Highway. Also a MR. TROUT, Continental Motors
gave a sighting.
MRS. MOONEY stated that nothing esse was said with the exception of the
above to indicate a Pacifist movement. She said that notning was mentionea au
this meeting which she could conscrue to be subversive in any way.
She saated that she intended to continue to attend meetings until she
determined whether or not the organization was communist and would continue to
report on the meetings to this office.

AN OPEN LETTER TO THE PRESIDENT OF THE UNITED STATES:
Mx, President:
Sept. 22, 1954
As citizens of Detroit and vicinity, we request audience with you regarding the coming
Space ships to tbis earth,
Many of us have seen these objects; so have others both in this
country and on foreign soil.
To us
It matters little whether these be called "UFO's" (unidentifled flying objects) space
ships, fireballs, flying saucers or any other name.
The point is that they are continuously
coming, and they are being observed both here and abroad.
It appears that our government with far greater facilities for observation than any indivi-
dual is perfectly aware of such phenomena, but has adopted a policy of silence and secrecy
in regard to them.
We understand that certain individuals within the Defense Department
are afraid of announcing the facts to the citizens for fear of panic.
Mr. President, we feel that this policy is false, fear-provoking and entirely mistaken for
the following reasons:
1. We believe that these phenomena are of a magnitude and importance such as to
warrant the utmost openness and cooperation between government and citizenry.
2. We believe that the present policy of silence and secrecy is a reflection on
the intelligence and loyalty of the American citizen.
3. We believe that this policy not only encourages fear and lack of confidence in
government, but. leads to exploitation by unscrupulous publishers and opportunistic
individuals who prey upon the natural curiosity of the American citizen.
4. We do not understand by what right certain public servants, utilizing the tax-
payers' facilities for information and observation, gather facts relative to
these phenomena, and then fail to properly inform the public.
5. Other countries have already acknowledged these phenomena and have publicly
appointed governmental commissions to investigate, collect information and report
to the people.
Mr. President: We, therefore, call upon you, our trusted leader, to utilize the great
powers of your office to change this shameful and unamerican policy of silence and secrecy.
We ask that our government come forward and make an honest and forthright acknowledgment
of these phenomena, and inform the citizens of such pertinent facts as are now available.
In return, the citizens then would be willing to report sightings and contacts which other-
wise would remain concealed because of fear of ridicule or rebuff by Government agencies.
This cooperation between the government and its citizenry would clear the atmosphere of fear
and suspicion, would renew confidence in our public officials, and pave the way to a better
understanding
and evaluation of these great phenomena.
Such a clear acknowledgment, emenating from the head of our nation might divert mankind's
attention from the spectre-of impending atomic war.
It could lift the consciousness of
everyone on this earth to a Cosmic level where a complete new set of Spiritual, scientific
and social values would be within the reach of all.
DETROIT FLYING SAUCER CLUB

STANDARD FORM NO. 84
Office Memorandum. UNITED STATES GOVERNMENT
:
SAC
DATE: NoV. 23, 1954
FROM :
SA CHARLES E. GENT (65-2677)
SUBJECT:
GEORGE ADANSHI, et al
ESPIONAGE
ALL INFORMATION CONTAINED
On lovenber 23, 1954 ur. BUD GOODMAN, reporter for
• the
Detroit free Press, called this office and talked to the writer
and stated he had some more material concerning the Flying Saucer
Club and he would be glad to send it over to us if we were as
interested in the organization as they thought we should be.
I advised him we would be interested in receiving any material he
or anyone else wished to submit.
Mir. GOODMAN then stated that his paper was interested
in running a story on the Flying Saucer Club and he wondered whether
they would be able to put a statement in it to the effect that
the FBI is investigating the organization.
The writer advised
Mir. 700DMAN that they should very definitely not make such a
statement inasmuch as it would be strictly conjectural on the
part of anyone who made it. Mr. GOODMAN then inquired whether
it would harm us if the free press ran a story on the organization
without making any reference to the Bureau.
The writer advised
Mr. GOODMAN that a reply to such a statement should most properly
come from Mr. MoINTIRE, the SAC, and I advised Mr. GOODMAN that
we would call him later concerning the matter.
After discussing this matter with the SAC, the writer
called GOOD AN at the City Desk of the Free Press and
advised him
that we haa no position in this matter and it would make no
difference one way or another to us whether the story was rur
by ris paper.
With respect to any statement concerning this
Bureau investigating the organization, the writer reiterated that
such a statement would be strictly conjectural.
Mr. GOODMAN stated that he appreciated the answers and
would also like to know whether he would be off base if he made
statement in his article concernin, wether the organization is
subversive.
The writer advised GOOD AN that this Bureau is not
at liberty to answer a question of that type and expected that
he would draw no conclusions in view of our inability to answer.

The enclosed copies of literature in my hands is being sent to
you at the request of Mr. O'Gara.
In each instance the stuff is mimeographed. The literature put
out under the name of Dr. Laughead's name has his name and address
stamped at the top, and is signed by him.
The other literature
has no signature or no letterheading. There is some merit to the
religious aspects invoked, but 1 sense a doctrine of tear inter.
woven in the material and
a doctrine of complacency, both of which
could possibly have an effect of neutralizing our young people on
basic Americanism.
My understanding with Mr. O'Gara was that my son from whom I got
this material) would not be approached or involved except through
He was just recently drawn into this because of his interest
in Flying Saucers. He has just finished five years in the Army.
I lost my other son last year on duty as a jet fighter pilot.
do not want to lose this one through what could be construed as
meddling on my part.
On the other hand, it is not at all imposs-
Ible that he could be enlisted to assist us, and certainly you car
count upon me to do anything within my capacity.
I may be unduly alarmed, but I do not like the thought that Flying
Saucer classes have been started on the campuses of many univer-
'sities and that this stuff is being_disseminated through them.
are best able to determine whether I am over-suspicious or not.
For credentials, the writer was chief assistant Secretary of State
of Michigan for two years, was Detroit's first inter-racial director,
and served three terms on the executive council of the Episcopal
diocese of Michigan. I mention this merely to avoid being labeled
a crackpot.
If I can serve you further, please do not hesitate to call upon me.

of the WEIDSON, who is a member of the abore club, advised that
one RANDALL COX, who is also a club member, has on
occasion stated:
to report to Washington, D.C. Ior a conference. Cos state that he
was supposed to bring with him one JOHN C. HOFFMAN, an officer ol
the above club and a cousin of DAVIDSON. COX has set the date for
his trip to Washington on several occasions, but when it came
time to go COX has stated that it was impossible for him to make
the trip and would go at another time.
DAVIDSON stated that he is a little dubious about being a
member of the above club as the club officers have no way of
accounting to the membership about the use of the club's funds.
DAVIDSON stated that he has no reason to suspect anyone of anything
but at times he has wondered about the sympathies,
with respect
to political views, of the following persons, HENRY MAYDAY and
GEORGE HUNT WILLIAMSON.
DAVIDSON stated that the purpose of the club was to study
all of the evidence and stories about flying saucers to see if they
are true or false and to try and keep the public informaed in such
a way as to prevent a riot while keeping the public informed.

Subject: Detroit Flying Saucer Club
Gentlemen:
Relative to our conversation with your Mr. Smith,
the follow-
ing things have transpired between myself and people of the Detroit
Flying Saucer Club
Thusada Janures 6, 1955
I met Mr. Henry Maday in a restaurant this evening.
By chance ne received my note regarding the letter I received
from Thissaly Miller in Clarkston, Michigan. He said he was awfully
not to do things like this - it was not right, nor was it according
to the MASTER'S plan. Then he remarked that the trouble with the
Michigan State College Professor's recent incident with the news-
papers and the lady in Chicago, was that only ONE person knew of
the things he advocated. And that was why it failed ITS NOT ACCORD-
ING TO THE MASTER'S PLAN THAI ONLY ONE PERSON HAS THE ANSWER AS TO
WHAT IS GOING TO TAKE PLACE.
I then told him about my losing my job. But unlike I explained
it to Mr. Smith

TO NORTHLAND
Dwell with us a while and refresh yourself!
A neighborly "Little Auditorium" Program Series heralding internationally
famous adventurers and speakers beckons you to a quality of listening tuned
to the original ear.

and your family can sit back comfortably, and travel the world over .....
within ten minutes driving and parking time of your home!
Henry Maday invites you to be present with your friends at this memor-

casion, Northland will be visited by that great man of the North, ADMIRAL

colorful background includes the following:
Assistant to Admiral Peary when he discovered
the North Pole!
Winner of Congressional Medals and National
Geographic Gold Medal!
Commander of thirty expeditions to the Arctic!
THE GREATEST LIVING ARCTIC AUTHORITY••
He has navigated his boat over 280, 000
miles in Arctic waters

this Arctic frontiersman...

Subject: Detroit Flying Saucer Club.
Gentlemen:
It has been some time since I have given you a report
on the Club. Although I have been in close contact with some
of the members nothing of a subversive nature to warrant a
report has taken place.
I have been with Mr. Maday on several of the programs
he has put on not in the interest of
• the Saucer Club. It
was apparently done for two reasons: To gain prestige for Mr.
Maday and possibly money. The MacMillan, Joe Fisher and John
Craig programs all were losers from a money standpoint.
There has been some squabbling between the Officers of
the saucer Club. It seems Marxer got ofi on a deal of her
own: showing the movie around
to the cities adjacent to Det-
roit,
this was not successful. John Hoffman has been dis-
satisfied with the way
• Maday has been doing_things and they
are disagreeing almost continuously mostly I suppose over
money and the fact that Maday rang Craig's picture of the
Bikini explosions. Randall Cox is critizing the lack of adver-
tizing etc., before each program, and so it goes.
The last meeting I attended
was the result of the last
Officers meeting. it was determined that an election should
take place and
that the Club should be reorganized.
The group
leaders were contacted. A meeting was held at the Veteran's
Memorial Building on February 25, 1955 at 8:00 p.m. (Letter
attached). About 28
the officers except fandall dox. a. andre together with all
a group leader
accused Hoffman of not allowing
• books to
be shown at a
meeting one night. It seems Hoffman told them it was none of
their business.

GEORGE ADAMSKI
MISCELLANEOUS INFORMATION CONCERNING
DATE: 4/25/55
FORMATION CONTAINEU
DATO 2A
• On 4/25/55, WILLIAM J. OHLAND, Wolverine Hotel, Detroit,
Mict nen, sevoy terested in the top on lie sanes rated
that he was very interested in the topic of flying saucers.
there was a very active organization in Detroit called the Detroit
Flying Saucer Club, which organization published a pamphlet periodically
called "The Vinama." He stated that this organization was sponsering
GEORGE ADAMSKI at a series of lectures on May 2; 3, 4, 1955 in Detroit.
The person who signed the mimeographed notice of these meetings is a
Mrs. LAURA MARXER, Logan 3-9074, Detroit, and under her signature were
the words,
"The Adamski Cormittee."
One of the books Mr. OHLAND had in his possession was a book
entitled, "Flying Saucers Have Landed" by DESMOND LESLIE and GEORGE
ADAMSKI.
Mr. OHLAND advised that he was furnishing the above information
in vien of the fact that one of the meetings to be held on May ? not
4, 1955, was to be a closed meeting, and he stated that he could
A-bomb should not be used in any future wars. He felt that this was
a possible front for a subversive group and desired it brought to the
attention of the Bureau.
ASAC CURTIS O. LYNUM, Minneapolis, personally recalled that
when he was assigned to the San Diego Division, that GEORGE ADAMSKI
two or three years ago made speeches in southern California relative
to having seen flying saucers. ADAMSKI was interviewed by FBI agents
and OSI agents and it is the
recollection of ASAC LYNUM that some of
the photographs of flying saucers which ADAMSKI had in his possession
were actually an old light fixture which was photographed out of focus
while spinning in the air.
The above is for the information
of the
Detroit and San Diego
Offices.
COLENJG
San Diege

DETROIT FLYING SAUCER
6432 Cass Avenue
Detroit 2, Michigan
TRinity 5-7300
CLUB
Dear friend or visitor of the Detroit Flying Saucer Club:
Your patience in waiting these long months between programs will have its just
reward. You are bound to us by "interstellar strands" that are closer and deeper than
even a paid membership, and we recognize our responsibility in this matter. Thereupon
we come to the near-end of the season with a most fitting climax.
DANIEL W. FRY, noted west coast author of "The White Sands Incident" is to be
our June 21st and 22nd speaker, at the Detroit Art Institute Auditorium, where he will
tell of his amazing experiences aboard a Flying saucer, which, remotely controlled
from a Mother Ship 500 miles up, took him from White Sands, New Mexico to New York
City and return, in less than an hour. This was in 1951, and since that date,
Dan Fry has had several outstanding experiences that will probably make his presen-
tation the greatest one yet' offered to members of our Club and their friends.
Make no mistake -- Dan Fry is no visionary dreamer, nor "psychic"; and his
experiences in a "U.F.O." was so valid and tangible that
many west coast scientists
have consulted with him for the unbelievable information which he has to disclose.
As a rocket technician expert at Aero-Jet in California, he commands an important
position where he has been employed for several years ... now on leave of absence to
present the Saucer Story as he sees it.
It is our exceptional good fortune to be
able to have him with us... to speak and to answer your questions at length.
For this reason we are scheduling this program on IWO NIGHIS: TUESDAY,
JUNE 21SI, and WEDNESDAY, JUNE 22ND, in the LARGE AUDITORIUM OF THE
DETROIT ART INSTITUE, corner Woodward at Kirby.
Tickets are $1.25 each,
which includes Amusement Tax, and may be procured NOW at GRINNELL,
BROTHERS, 1515 Woodward Avenue, or at thé door after 7:00 p.m. Time of
Program - 8:15 p.m.
Important announcements will also be made... two major sightings... and Summer
plans for the Club. Ihe June issue of the VIMANA will be ready and on sale... the
best issue to date, almost twice as thick as formerly.
FINALLY... do not miss BUD GUEST'S INTERVIEW WITH DAN FRY on MONDAY evening,
June 13th between 7:00 and 7:30 over WJR Radio; and ROSS MULHOLLAND'S afternoon
interview exactly one week later, over WWJ Radio, at 1:00 p.I. Monday, June 20th,
after Fry returns from an interval trip to Cleveland.
BE SURE AND TELL YOUR FRIENDS OF THESE BROADCASTS!
Hoping to see you right along with us, and the very best of everything in the
meantime, I am,
ALL INFORMATION CONTAINED
DEES2/85
Most cordially,
braced

She/form
advised he has been attending lectures and meetings sponsored
BYS
that "flying saucers" actually exist. He said that as far as he
was concerned, up to this time there had never been any indica-
tion that captioned group was not completely loyal to the U.s.
However, on 7/26/55, captioned group sponsored a lecture by one
BUCK NEISON, who stated, among other things, that "the form of
govt under which we live in the U.s. is wrong and should be des-
troyed, along with all govt bldgs" and also "that our whole con-
cept of Christianity is wrong and the Bible should be destroyed".
Mr. GRAY stated he became quite upset and arranged a private
interview with BUCK NEISON, which interview he recorded on a tape
recorder, and during which, NELSON allegedly reiterated the above
two statements concerning the U.s. and the Bible.
MI. GRAY stated he wanted to furnish this tape recording and other
tape recordings he has
taken, to the FBI in order that their contents
be properly evaluated.
we Arrangements were made for Mr. GRAY to bring his tape record-
ings and whatever other info he might have concerning captioned
group to the Detroit. Office at 4:00 pm on 8/1/55.
Mr. GRAY stated the following individuals were connected with
captioned group:
DANIEL W. FRI
BUCK NEISON
while the following
persons were associated with the Interplanetary Study Group (an
off-shoot of captioned group):
Mrs. IAURA MARXER
GEORGE ADAMSKI
Indices negative re GRAY and NEISON but FRY, MARXER and ADAMSKI
are frequently mentioned in instant case.
*** Suggest SA familiar with captioned group interview GRAY and
listen to his tape recordings to determine whether or not info
of a subversive nature is being disseminated by the speakers
sponsored by captioned group.

STANDARD FORM NO. 64
Office Memorandum . UNITED STATES GOVERNMENT
TO
: SAC
DATE: AUGUSI 8, 1955
FROM : SA JOHN I. SHELBURNE (65-2677)
ALL INFOBMATION CONTAINED
SUBJECT: DETROIT FIYING SAUCER CLUB
Espionage - X
On August 6, 1955, SA JOHN I. SHELBURNE was contacted by
SA JAMES C. HOLMES
who advised that CAPTAIN CUNNION, OSI, USAF,
Selfridge, Mt. Clemens, Michizan desired to contact him concerning
the above organization.
SA. JOHN L• SHELBURNE immediately contacted CAPTAIN CUNNION
by telephone. He stated that he had just received a telephone
call from MARTINE GRAY who had stated that he had contacted
this office and furnished information concerning a lecture given
by BUCK NELSON CUNNION said that he was to interview GRAY on
August 8, 1955 and hear the tape
recordings of the Nelson lecture
and the interview that he had with him. CUNNION desired any
information that would aid him in his investigation of unidentified
flying objects®
He was advised that GRAY
had been interviewed and that
the recordings which he had were footed by SA SHELBURNE and
from the information thereon the Bureau at the present time had no
interest in the case.
*
CUNNION stated that if in his interview with GRAY he detarmined
anything that might be of interest to this Office that he would
advise ASAC FULIS.

Ball of Fire
Lights Sky
Over State
GRAND RAPIDS, Sept. 6.-An
"unidentified flying object, prob-i
ably a meteor, flashed across the*
Skies of Western Michigan last
night.
Police stations, airport control
towers and the Air Force Filter
Center here received reports of
the object from Muskegon, Rock-
ford, Grand Rapids,
Caledonia,
Cutlerville and Sparta.
It was
sighted in all those, places at
abouf the same time.
CALLED BALL OF FIRE
Ong man described it as a ball
of fire
"as large as a basketbäl!."
Another said it, looked like a
burning airplane. Several said
it appeared to be falling. Others
said it seemed to move slowly
northward across the sky.
It was blue, greenish, or firey,
depending upon who saw it
saucer."
No one reported it as a "flying"

FACTS OF COMPLAINT:
Complainant called at 5:08 PM, 9-11-55 to advise that there was a white,
florescent, round object in the skies west of his residence at an
altitude of about 10,000 feet, just slightly above the clouds. He
stated that he had been watching it for about 3/4 of an hour and
that it had not moved or drifted, nor did it appear to be anchored.
He stated that he was an former aviator and that it appeared quite un-
usual to him.

by the United States was printed.

and riot.

jurisdiction or responsibility in releasing information
of this type for publication.

information appeared in a regular bound book.

the service that had developed the missile such as the Air
army or Navy, to determine what data pertaining to
the missile could be released for publication.

wanted to use this material for any type of publication

ASTE
INFORMATION
according to a report in the betroit times, waron y
(a copy of wnich is enclosed for your convenience),
FBI agents from the Flint, Michigan office viewed a
color photograph of an unidentified flying object (UFO),
then took the photograph for analysis.
Ine photograph in
question wis taken by Mr. Joe Perry and reportedly showed
a disc-shaped object with a dome, silhouetted against
the moon.
In a letter last year you stated that investigation of
UFOs was not within the jurisdiction of the Bureau, and
that the Air Force is the responsible agency.
In view of
the Detroit Times report, we would appreciate having answers
the following questions:
(1).
Has there been a change in FBI policy in regard to UFO
investigation since last year?
(2) It not, does the FBI gather evidence of uFos on behalf
of the Air Force land does this explain the action by FBI
agents in the case of the color photograph)?
(3) • If the above-mentioned photograph has been analyzed in
FBI Laboratories, or for FBI purposes,
may we have a copy
of the analysis report and be informed of the final disposition
. of the photograph?
Sincerely yours,

colored transparency of the moon and a unidentified
flying object for identification.. Up to this point I

Air Force Says
Saucer Picture
Mere Film Flaw
GRAND BLANC-Is it a Visiting!
Venusian or a pictorial polyp?
Joe Perry still isn't sure, but
he wants his picture back.
As related in an earlier story,
Joe,
an amateur astronomei,
took a routine picture of the
moon some time ago. Later, he
was amazed to see what appear-
ed to be a Flying Saucer on the
film, outlined against the moon.
Joe called the FBI and his pic-
ture began a tortuous journey
from Flint to Washington to De-
troit to Mt. Clemens and now ap-
pears headed for Dayton, Ohio,
and the Air Force Technical In-
telligence Center there.
..When
the
picture finally
found its way to the Office of
Special Investigation at Selfrige
Air Force Base in Mt. Clemens,
Joe contacted the Review to ask
for help in finding out what was
being done with it. A Review re-
porter contacted the base and
was told by a Major Cooper-
smith that the picture was
"of
no significance" and that the
Flying Saucer was "obviously a
flaw in the film."
No Picture For Joe
Asked if Joe could have his
picture now; the Major replied
that ho would get it eventually
but first it would be sent to Ohio
together with his report.
"Will the report be made pub-
lic?" the reporter asked,
"No," said the Major.
"How come a film flaw rates
a report and shuttling to an in-
melligetre center?" the reporeer
(asked.
"These things must be done.
accordinging to procedure," said
the Major.
Joe isn't the only one concern-
ed with the fate of his picture.
¡ Major Donald Keyhoe,
former
Air Force member and now head
of a group investigating reports
of UFO's (unidentified flying ob-
jects to the unitiated) has been
in close touch
with the Grand
Blanc restaurant owner. Bitter
in his indictment of the military
handling of UFO's, Keyhoe pre-
viously had warned Joe that he
might never
see
his picture
again. He cautioned against de-
manding the picture before of-
ficials had plenty of time to ex-
amine it.
In the light of recent develop.
ments, Joe says he is going to!
call Keyhoe and find out what
he should do know.
In the meantime, a lot of peo
ple are wondering whether Joe's
saucer is above ... or a blot.
If there are
any Venusians
jouna June in next week fo
ulther developments~
